People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… I came in because I’ve been having this feeling that I just can’t seem to shake, even when I’m having a pretty good day otherwise. It’s mostly around people from church, and also the walking club I go to. I keep feeling like certain people are watching me, not in an obvious way exactly, but like they’re keeping track of me.

And then little things start to seem like they’re about me. Like something written in the church bulletin, or a comment someone makes in passing during the walk, and I’ll think, “That was meant for me,” or “They’re trying to send a message about me without saying it directly.” I know it probably sounds strange, and I’ve tried to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into things, but it keeps happening, and it’s been upsetting enough that I thought I should talk to someone.

It feels more like… connected, I guess. I don’t know that I can prove they’re all sitting down together and planning it, but it doesn’t feel completely random or just one person, either. It’s like certain people at church and then certain people from the walking club seem to pick up on the same thing.

Like I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a little note, and it feels like it’s meant for me, like they put it there so I’d know they were aware of me. And then later, during the walking group, someone will say something in passing that seems to line up with it, not direct enough that I could point to it and say, “There, that’s it,” but enough that I get that feeling again, like they’re watching me or keeping track.

I know that sounds… I know how it sounds. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into it. But when it happens in both places, church and the walking club, it’s hard not to feel like there’s some kind of connection between them.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I suppose… yes and no. I mean, I know other people might not understand why a church bulletin would feel like it’s talking about me, or why some offhand comment in the walking club would seem connected. If I tried to explain it to someone, they might say, “Well, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They were just making conversation.”

But to me, in the moment, it feels pointed. Like even on days when I’m doing all right otherwise, I can’t quite shake this feeling that certain people are watching me, sort of keeping tabs, and then those little things — a phrase in the bulletin, something someone says during the walk — feel like messages about me. Not loud or obvious, but like they’re meant for me to notice.

I do realize it probably sounds unusual to someone else. That’s part of why I’m here, because I can hear how it might sound, but it still keeps feeling real to me when it happens.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not threatening like they’re going to hurt me, exactly… I don’t usually think that. It’s more that it feels uncomfortable and pointed, like they’re watching me and trying to let me know they know things about me, or they’re talking about me without saying my name.

Like with the church bulletin, it might just be a phrase or a little note, but I’ll read it and think, “That’s about me.” And then later someone in the walking club says something that seems to match up with it, and I get this sinking feeling, like it isn’t just coincidence. It doesn’t feel openly dangerous, but it does feel unsettling, like I’m being monitored or singled out.

Even on days when I’m otherwise doing okay, I can’t shake it. So I wouldn’t say threatening in a direct way, but it does make me feel uneasy and guarded.

manifestation: There are mornings when I find myself standing by the front door with my purse in my hand for a long time, not going out and not really knowing what I was about to do.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I don't talk much at coffee hour anymore, and even when someone tells a funny story I just sit there with a flat face like the feeling can't get out.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to love my garden, quilting, and reading before bed, but now I mostly leave the quilt basket untouched and the weeds can grow without me caring much.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Most days food just doesn't appeal to me, so I pick at toast or soup and my skirts have been hanging looser than they used to.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even after a full night in bed, carrying laundry or walking to the mailbox can make my body feel heavy, like I've already done a day's work.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep going over every little thing I might have done wrong, especially not calling my friend more, and I feel like I must have failed her and everybody else.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but I catch myself thinking more and more that it might be all right if I went to sleep and didn't wake up.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was at my lowest, barely eating and thinking I had failed everyone, I was also hearing my friend's voice and believing church people were sending messages about me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: For most of this whole stretch, the sadness, guilt, and loss of interest have been there in the background, even during times when the voices or suspicious thoughts were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a spell of several weeks when I was getting dressed, eating a bit better, and not feeling so weighed down, but I still heard my name in the house and thought people at church were hinting about me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I still live at home, but I have stopped keeping up with quilting gatherings, walking club, and much of my gardening, and my daughter has started checking on my meals and appointments more often.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on since not long after my close friend died, about a year and five months ago.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The voices, messages, and trouble keeping my thoughts straight have been happening for about eleven months now.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The deep sadness, guilt, and loss of interest have been with me for about a year and two months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think these people who you feel are watching you are working together or is it more individual?\n\nPatient: It feels more like… connected, I guess. I don’t know that I can prove they’re all sitting down together and planning it, but it doesn’t feel completely random or just one person, either. It’s like certain people at church and then certain people from the walking club seem to pick up on the same thing.\n\nLike I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a little note, and it feels like it’s meant for me, like they put it there so I’d know they were aware of me. And then later, during the walking group, someone will say something in passing that seems to line up with it, not direct enough that I could point to it and say, “There, that’s it,” but enough that I get that feeling again, like they’re watching me or keeping track.\n\nI know that sounds… I know how it sounds. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into it. But when it happens in both places, church and the walking club, it’s hard not to feel like there’s some kind of connection between them.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages you think are being sent to you are in any way unusual or impossible for others to understand?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes and no. I mean, I know other people might not understand why a church bulletin would feel like it’s talking about me, or why some offhand comment in the walking club would seem connected. If I tried to explain it to someone, they might say, “Well, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They were just making conversation.”\n\nBut to me, in the moment, it feels pointed. Like even on days when I’m doing all right otherwise, I can’t quite shake this feeling that certain people are watching me, sort of keeping tabs, and then those little things — a phrase in the bulletin, something someone says during the walk — feel like messages about me. Not loud or obvious, but like they’re meant for me to notice.\n\nI do realize it probably sounds unusual to someone else. That’s part of why I’m here, because I can hear how it might sound, but it still keeps feeling real to me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages you think are being sent to you are threatening in any way?\n\nPatient: Not threatening like they’re going to hurt me, exactly… I don’t usually think that. It’s more that it feels uncomfortable and pointed, like they’re watching me and trying to let me know they know things about me, or they’re talking about me without saying my name.\n\nLike with the church bulletin, it might just be a phrase or a little note, but I’ll read it and think, “That’s about me.” And then later someone in the walking club says something that seems to match up with it, and I get this sinking feeling, like it isn’t just coincidence. It doesn’t feel openly dangerous, but it does feel unsettling, like I’m being monitored or singled out.\n\nEven on days when I’m otherwise doing okay, I can’t shake it. So I wouldn’t say threatening in a direct way, but it does make me feel uneasy and guarded.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow long have you been experiencing these feelings of being watched and receiving hidden messages?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. It’s been… about eleven months now, I think. Not quite a full year, but close enough that it feels like it’s been going on for a long time.

And during that same stretch, I’ve also had the voices and times where it’s harder to keep my thoughts straight, like I get tangled up trying to explain myself.

work.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep going over every little thing I might have done wrong, especially not calling my friend more, and I feel like I must have failed her and everybody else.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but I catch myself thinking more and more that it might be all right if I went to sleep and didn't wake up.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was at my lowest, barely eating and thinking I had failed everyone, I was also hearing my friend's voice and believing church people were sending messages about me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a spell of several weeks when I was getting dressed, eating a bit better, and not feeling so weighed down, but I still heard my name in depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The deep sadness, guilt, and loss of interest have been with me for about a year and two months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages you think are being sent to you are in any way unusual or impossible for others to understand?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes and no. I mean, I know other people might not understand why a church bulletin would feel like it’s talking about me, or why some offhand comment in the walking club would seem connected. If I tried to explain it to someone, they might say, “Well, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They were just making conversation.”\n\nBut to me, in the moment, it feels pointed. Like even on days when I’m doing all right otherwise, I can’t quite shake this feeling that certain people are watching me, sort of keeping tabs, and then those little things — a phrase in the bulletin, something someone says during the walk — feel like messages about me. Not loud or obvious, but like they’re meant for me to notice.\n\nI do realize it probably sounds unusual to someone else. That’s part of why I’m here, because I can hear how it might sound, but it still keeps feeling real to me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages you think are being sent to you are threatening in any way?\n\nPatient: Not threatening like they’re going to hurt me, exactly… I don’t usually think that. It’s more that it feels uncomfortable and pointed, like they’re watching me and trying to let me know they know things about me, or they’re talking about me without saying my name.\n\nLike with the church bulletin, it might just be a phrase or a little note, but I’ll read it and think, “That’s about me.” And then later someone in the walking club says something that seems to match up with it, and I get this sinking feeling, like it isn’t just coincidence. It doesn’t feel openly dangerous, but it does feel unsettling, like I’m being monitored or singled out.\n\nEven on days when I’m otherwise doing okay, I can’t shake it. So I wouldn’t say threatening in a direct way, but it does make me feel uneasy and guarded.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of being watched and receiving hidden messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about eleven months now, I think. Not quite a full year, but close enough that it feels like it’s been going on for a long time.\n\nAt first I wasn’t sure what to make of it, and I kept thinking maybe it was just me being too sensitive or reading things wrong. But the feeling of being watched, and then those messages or little signs that seem like they’re meant for me, that’s been happening for around eleven months. And during that same stretch, I’ve also had the voices and times where it’s harder to keep my thoughts straight, like I get tangled up trying to explain myself.\n\nSo, yes… I’d say roughly eleven months.\n\n[Last Question]\nYou mentioned having voices, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… by voices, I don’t mean like a whole conversation going on all day or anything like that. It’s more… it happens sometimes when the house is very quiet, especially if I’m alone and there isn’t any television or music on.

I’ll hear my name, clear enough that I look up or even answer, like, “What?” And then I remember nobody is there. Sometimes it’s just a few words, not a long sentence. The strange part is it can sound like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or maybe the porch. It’s not muffled the way you’d think if I was just imagining it — it feels clear in that moment, like someone really called from the other room.

Then I’ll go still and listen, and of course there’s no one there. I feel embarrassed even saying it, because I know how it sounds, but it’s happened enough times that I can’t just brush it off as a one-time mistake. It startles me more than anything. Sometimes I answer before I even have time to think, and then I feel silly standing there in the quiet.

Like something written in the church bulletin, or a comment someone makes in passing during the walk, and I’ll think, “That was meant for me,” or “They’re trying to send a message about me without saying it directly.” I know it probably sounds strange, and I’ve tried to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into things, but it keeps happening, and it’s been upsetting enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people who you feel are watching you are working together or is it more individual?\n\nPatient: It feels more like… connected, I guess. untouched and the weeds can grow without me caring much.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Most days food just doesn't appeal to me, so I pick at toast or soup and my skirts have been hanging looser than they used to.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even after a full night in bed, carrying laundry or walking to the mailbox can make my body feel heavy, like I've already done a day's work.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep going over every little thing I might have done wrong, especially not calling my friend more, and I feel like I must have failed her and everybody else.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but I catch myself thinking more and more that it might be all right if I went to sleep and didn't wake up.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was at my lowest, barely eating and thinking I had failed everyone, I was also hearing my friend's voice and believing church people were sending messages about me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: For most of this whole stretch, the sadness, guilt, and loss of interest have been there in the background, even during times when the voices or suspicious thoughts were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a spell of several weeks when I was getting dressed, eating a bit better, and not feeling so weighed down, but I still heard my name in the house and thought people at church were hinting about me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I still live at home, but I have stopped keeping up with quilting gatherings, walking club, and much of my gardening, and my daughter has started checking on my meals and appointments more often.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on since not long after my close friend died, about a year and five months ago.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The voices, messages, and trouble keeping my thoughts straight have been happening for about eleven months now.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The deep sadness, guilt, and loss of interest have been with me for about a year and two months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages you think are being sent to you are threatening in any way?\n\nPatient: Not threatening like they’re going to hurt me, exactly… I don’t usually think that. It’s more that it feels uncomfortable and pointed, like they’re watching me and trying to let me know they know things about me, or they’re talking about me without saying my name.\n\nLike with the church bulletin, it might just be a phrase or a little note, but I’ll read it and think, “That’s about me.” And then later someone in the walking club says something that seems to match up with it, and I get this sinking feeling, like it isn’t just coincidence. It doesn’t feel openly dangerous, but it does feel unsettling, like I’m being monitored or singled out.\n\nEven on days when I’m otherwise doing okay, I can’t shake it. So I wouldn’t say threatening in a direct way, but it does make me feel uneasy and guarded.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of being watched and receiving hidden messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about eleven months now, I think. Not quite a full year, but close enough that it feels like it’s been going on for a long time.\n\nAt first I wasn’t sure what to make of it, and I kept thinking maybe it was just me being too sensitive or reading things wrong. But the feeling of being watched, and then those messages or little signs that seem like they’re meant for me, that’s been happening for around eleven months. And during that same stretch, I’ve also had the voices and times where it’s harder to keep my thoughts straight, like I get tangled up trying to explain myself.\n\nSo, yes… I’d say roughly eleven months.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned having voices, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Yes… by voices, I don’t mean like a whole conversation going on all day or anything like that. It’s more… it happens sometimes when the house is very quiet, especially if I’m alone and there isn’t any television or music on.\n\nI’ll hear my name, clear enough that I look up or even answer, like, “What?” And then I remember nobody is there. Sometimes it’s just a few words, not a long sentence. The strange part is it can sound like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or maybe the porch. It’s not muffled the way you’d think if I was just imagining it — it feels clear in that moment, like someone really called from the other room.\n\nThen I’ll go still and listen, and of course there’s no one there. I feel embarrassed even saying it, because I know how it sounds, but it’s happened enough times that I can’t just brush it off as a one-time mistake. It startles me more than anything. Sometimes I answer before I even have time to think, and then I feel silly standing there in the quiet.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo the voices you hear ever tell you to do something or influence your actions in any way?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really in the sense of giving me instructions, no. It’s not like a voice says, “Go do this,” or “Don’t do that.” It’s more that I’ll hear my name, or just a few words, and it catches me off guard.

Usually it’s when the house is very quiet, and I’ll think I hear my friend’s voice from the kitchen or the porch, clear enough that I might answer before I remember nobody is there. Like I’ll say, “What?” and then I’m standing there listening, and there’s nothing. That’s the part that bothers me, how real it seems for a second.

I suppose it influences me only in that I stop what I’m doing and check, or I get a little unsettled afterward. But it doesn’t tell me to do things. It’s more startling than commanding.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n7\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23, F28\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I came in because I’ve been having this feeling that I just can’t seem to shake, even when I’m having a pretty good day otherwise. It’s mostly around people from church, and also the walking club I go to. I keep feeling like certain people are watching me, not in an obvious way exactly, but like they’re keeping track of me.\n\nAnd then little things start to seem like they’re about me. Like something written in the church bulletin, or a comment someone makes in passing during the walk, and I’ll think, “That was meant for me,” or “They’re trying to send a message about me without saying it directly.” I know it probably sounds strange, and I’ve tried to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into things, but it keeps happening, and it’s been upsetting enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people who you feel are watching you are working together or is it more individual?\n\nPatient: It feels more like… connected, I guess. I don’t know that I can prove they’re all sitting down together and planning it, but it doesn’t feel completely random or just one person, either. It’s like certain people at church and then certain people from the walking club seem to pick up on the same thing.\n\nLike I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a little note, and it feels like it’s meant for me, like they put it there so I’d know they were aware of me. And then later, during the walking group, someone will say something in passing that seems to line up with it, not direct enough that I could point to it and say, “There, that’s it,” but enough that I get that feeling again, like they’re watching me or keeping track.\n\nI know that sounds… I know how it sounds. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into it. But when it happens in both places, church and the walking club, it’s hard not to feel like there’s some kind of connection between them.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages you think are being sent to you are in any way unusual or impossible for others to understand?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes and no. I mean, I know other people might not understand why a church bulletin would feel like it’s talking about me, or why some offhand comment in the walking club would seem connected. If I tried to explain it to someone, they might say, “Well, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They were just making conversation.”\n\nBut to me, in the moment, it feels pointed. Like even on days when I’m doing all right otherwise, I can’t quite shake this feeling that certain people are watching me, sort of keeping tabs, and then those little things — a phrase in the bulletin, something someone says during the walk — feel like messages about me. Not loud or obvious, but like they’re meant for me to notice.\n\nI do realize it probably sounds unusual to someone else. That’s part of why I’m here, because I can hear how it might sound, but it still keeps feeling real to me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages you think are being sent to you are threatening in any way?\n\nPatient: Not threatening like they’re going to hurt me, exactly… I don’t usually think that. It’s more that it feels uncomfortable and pointed, like they’re watching me and trying to let me know they know things about me, or they’re talking about me without saying my name.\n\nLike with the church bulletin, it might just be a phrase or a little note, but I’ll read it and think, “That’s about me.” And then later someone in the walking club says something that seems to match up with it, and I get this sinking feeling, like it isn’t just coincidence. It doesn’t feel openly dangerous, but it does feel unsettling, like I’m being monitored or singled out.\n\nEven on days when I’m otherwise doing okay, I can’t shake it. So I wouldn’t say threatening in a direct way, but it does make me feel uneasy and guarded.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of being watched and receiving hidden messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about eleven months now, I think. It’s more… it happens sometimes when the house is very quiet, especially if I’m alone and there isn’t any television or music on.\n\nI’ll hear my name, clear enough that I look up or even answer, like, “What?” And then I remember nobody is there. Sometimes it’s just a few words, not a long sentence. The strange part is it can sound like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or maybe the porch. It’s not muffled the way you’d think if I was just imagining it — it feels clear in that moment, like someone really called from the other room.\n\nThen I’ll go still and listen, and of course there’s no one there. I feel embarrassed even saying it, because I know how it sounds, but it’s happened enough times that I can’t just brush it off as a one-time mistake. It startles me more than anything. Sometimes I answer before I even have time to think, and then I feel silly standing there in the quiet.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear ever tell you to do something or influence your actions in any way?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of giving me instructions, no. ---------- turn 8 | patient:alignment | patient.build_alignment_messages() | model=gpt-5.5 ----------
========== INPUT [patient] ==========

Sometimes it’s just a few words, not a long sentence. The strange part is it can sound like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or maybe the porch. Like I’ll say, “What?” and then I’m standing there listening, and there’s nothing. That’s the part that bothers me, how real it seems for a second.\n\nI suppose it influences me only in that I stop what I’m doing and check, or I get a little unsettled afterward. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think that’s part of what scares me, because it does start to feel like it all fits together, even when I tell myself maybe I’m misunderstanding.

It’s mostly connected to people at church and in my walking club. On better days, I can still go and act normal enough, but I can’t shake this feeling that certain people are watching me more closely than they should be. Like they notice where I sit, who I talk to, whether I missed a Sunday, little things like that.

And then I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a line that seems too close to something about me, and I think, “Why would they put that in there unless it was meant for me?” Or someone in the walking club will say something kind of casual, but the way they say it feels pointed, like there’s a hidden message underneath it. Not always direct, but enough that I feel like they’re trying to tell me they know things about me or they’re discussing me without saying it outright.

I know if I say it out loud it sounds like maybe I’m reading too much into things. I do know that. But in the moment, it feels connected, like the bulletin and the comments and the watching are all part of the same thing. Even when I’m having a better day, I can’t completely talk myself out of it.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n8\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23, F28\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I came in because I’ve been having this feeling that I just can’t seem to shake, even when I’m having a pretty good day otherwise. It’s mostly around people from church, and also the walking club I go to. I keep feeling like certain people are watching me, not in an obvious way exactly, but like they’re keeping track of me.\n\nAnd then little things start to seem like they’re about me. Like something written in the church bulletin, or a comment someone makes in passing during the walk, and I’ll think, “That was meant for me,” or “They’re trying to send a message about me without saying it directly.” I know it probably sounds strange, and I’ve tried to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into things, but it keeps happening, and it’s been upsetting enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people who you feel are watching you are working together or is it more individual?\n\nPatient: It feels more like… connected, I guess. I don’t know that I can prove they’re all sitting down together and planning it, but it doesn’t feel completely random or just one person, either. It’s like certain people at church and then certain people from the walking club seem to pick up on the same thing.\n\nLike I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a little note, and it feels like it’s meant for me, like they put it there so I’d know they were aware of me. And then later, during the walking group, someone will say something in passing that seems to line up with it, not direct enough that I could point to it and say, “There, that’s it,” but enough that I get that feeling again, like they’re watching me or keeping track.\n\nI know that sounds… I know how it sounds. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into it. But when it happens in both places, church and the walking club, it’s hard not to feel like there’s some kind of connection between them.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages you think are being sent to you are in any way unusual or impossible for others to understand?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes and no. I mean, I know other people might not understand why a church bulletin would feel like it’s talking about me, or why some offhand comment in the walking club would seem connected. If I tried to explain it to someone, they might say, “Well, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They were just making conversation.”\n\nBut to me, in the moment, it feels pointed. Like even on days when I’m doing all right otherwise, I can’t quite shake this feeling that certain people are watching me, sort of keeping tabs, and then those little things — a phrase in the bulletin, something someone says during the walk — feel like messages about me. Not loud or obvious, but like they’re meant for me to notice.\n\nI do realize it probably sounds unusual to someone else. That’s part of why I’m here, because I can hear how it might sound, but it still keeps feeling real to me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages you think are being sent to you are threatening in any way?\n\nPatient: Not threatening like they’re going to hurt me, exactly… I don’t usually think that. It’s more that it feels uncomfortable and pointed, like they’re watching me and trying to let me know they know things about me, or they’re talking about me without saying my name.\n\nLike with the church bulletin, it might just be a phrase or a little note, but I’ll read it and think, “That’s about me.” And then later someone in the walking club says something that seems to match up with it, and I get this sinking feeling, like it isn’t just coincidence. It doesn’t feel openly dangerous, but it does feel unsettling, like I’m being monitored or singled out.\n\nEven on days when I’m otherwise doing okay, I can’t shake it. So I wouldn’t say threatening in a direct way, but it does make me feel uneasy and guarded.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of being watched and receiving hidden messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about eleven months now, I think. Not quite a full year, but close enough that it feels like it’s been going on for a long time.\n\nAt first I wasn’t sure what to make of it, and I kept thinking maybe it was just me being too sensitive or reading things wrong. But the feeling of being watched, and then those messages or little signs that seem like they’re meant for me, that’s been happening for around eleven months. And during that same stretch, I’ve also had the voices and times where it’s harder to keep my thoughts straight, like I get tangled up trying to explain myself.\n\nSo, yes… I’d say roughly eleven months.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned having voices, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Yes… by voices, I don’t mean like a whole conversation going on all day or anything like that. It’s more… it happens sometimes when the house is very quiet, especially if I’m alone and there isn’t any television or music on.\n\nI’ll hear my name, clear enough that I look up or even answer, like, “What?” And then I remember nobody is there. Sometimes it’s just a few words, not a long sentence. The strange part is it can sound like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or maybe the porch. It’s not muffled the way you’d think if I was just imagining it — it feels clear in that moment, like someone really called from the other room.\n\nThen I’ll go still and listen, and of course there’s no one there. I feel embarrassed even saying it, because I know how it sounds, but it’s happened enough times that I can’t just brush it off as a one-time mistake. It startles me more than anything. Sometimes I answer before I even have time to think, and then I feel silly standing there in the quiet.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear ever tell you to do something or influence your actions in any way?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of giving me instructions, no. It’s not like a voice says, “Go do this,” or “Don’t do that.” It’s more that I’ll hear my name, or just a few words, and it catches me off guard.\n\nUsually it’s when the house is very quiet, and I’ll think I hear my friend’s voice from the kitchen or the porch, clear enough that I might answer before I remember nobody is there. Like I’ll say, “What?” and then I’m standing there listening, and there’s nothing. That’s the part that bothers me, how real it seems for a second.\n\nI suppose it influences me only in that I stop what I’m doing and check, or I get a little unsettled afterward. But it doesn’t tell me to do things. It’s more startling than commanding.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of being watched and the messages you think you're receiving seem to be part of a larger story or idea that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s part of what scares me, because it does start to feel like it all fits together, even when I tell myself maybe I’m misunderstanding.\n\nIt’s mostly connected to people at church and in my walking club. On better days, I can still go and act normal enough, but I can’t shake this feeling that certain people are watching me more closely than they should be. Like they notice where I sit, who I talk to, whether I missed a Sunday, little things like that.\n\nAnd then I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a line that seems too close to something about me, and I think, “Why would they put that in there unless it was meant for me?” Or someone in the walking club will say something kind of casual, but the way they say it feels pointed, like there’s a hidden message underneath it. Not always direct, but enough that I feel like they’re trying to tell me they know things about me or they’re discussing me without saying it outright.\n\nI know if I say it out loud it sounds like maybe I’m reading too much into things. I do know that. But in the moment, it feels connected, like the bulletin and the comments and the watching are all part of the same thing. Even when I’m having a better day, I can’t completely talk myself out of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It doesn’t feel openly dangerous, but it does feel unsettling, like I’m being monitored or singled out.\n\nEven on days when I’m otherwise doing okay, I can’t shake it. So I wouldn’t say threatening in a direct way, but it does make me feel uneasy and guarded.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of being watched and receiving hidden messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about eleven months now, I think. Not quite a full year, but close enough that it feels like it’s been going on for a long time.\n\nAt first I wasn’t sure what to make of it, and I kept thinking maybe it was just me being too sensitive or reading things wrong. But the feeling of being watched, and then those messages or little signs that seem like they’re meant for me, that’s been happening for around eleven months. And during that same stretch, I’ve also had the voices and times where it’s harder to keep my thoughts straight, like I get tangled up trying to explain myself.\n\nSo, yes… I’d say roughly eleven months.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned having voices, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Yes… by voices, I don’t mean like a whole conversation going on all day or anything like that. It’s more… it happens sometimes when the house is very quiet, especially if I’m alone and there isn’t any television or music on.\n\nI’ll hear my name, clear enough that I look up or even answer, like, “What?” And then I remember nobody is there. Sometimes it’s just a few words, not a long sentence. The strange part is it can sound like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or maybe the porch. It’s not muffled the way you’d think if I was just imagining it — it feels clear in that moment, like someone really called from the other room.\n\nThen I’ll go still and listen, and of course there’s no one there. I feel embarrassed even saying it, because I know how it sounds, but it’s happened enough times that I can’t just brush it off as a one-time mistake. It startles me more than anything. Sometimes I answer before I even have time to think, and then I feel silly standing there in the quiet.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear ever tell you to do something or influence your actions in any way?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of giving me instructions, no. It’s not like a voice says, “Go do this,” or “Don’t do that.” It’s more that I’ll hear my name, or just a few words, and it catches me off guard.\n\nUsually it’s when the house is very quiet, and I’ll think I hear my friend’s voice from the kitchen or the porch, clear enough that I might answer before I remember nobody is there. Like I’ll say, “What?” and then I’m standing there listening, and there’s nothing. That’s the part that bothers me, how real it seems for a second.\n\nI suppose it influences me only in that I stop what I’m doing and check, or I get a little unsettled afterward. But it doesn’t tell me to do things. It’s more startling than commanding.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of being watched and the messages you think you're receiving seem to be part of a larger story or idea that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s part of what scares me, because it does start to feel like it all fits together, even when I tell myself maybe I’m misunderstanding.\n\nIt’s mostly connected to people at church and in my walking club. On better days, I can still go and act normal enough, but I can’t shake this feeling that certain people are watching me more closely than they should be. Like they notice where I sit, who I talk to, whether I missed a Sunday, little things like that.\n\nAnd then I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a line that seems too close to something about me, and I think, “Why would they put that in there unless it was meant for me?” Or someone in the walking club will say something kind of casual, but the way they say it feels pointed, like there’s a hidden message underneath it. Not always direct, but enough that I feel like they’re trying to tell me they know things about me or they’re discussing me without saying it outright.\n\nI know if I say it out loud it sounds like maybe I’m reading too much into things. I do know that. But in the moment, it feels connected, like the bulletin and the comments and the watching are all part of the same thing. Even when I’m having a better day, I can’t completely talk myself out of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Sometimes I can distract myself for a little while, yes. If I’m busy with something very ordinary, like washing dishes or talking to someone about nothing important, I may not be thinking about it every minute.

But honestly, even on my better days, it’s still there in the background. That’s actually why I came in, because I can’t seem to fully shake this feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, sort of keeping track of me. And then little things feel like messages — something in the bulletin, or a comment someone makes while we’re walking — and I start thinking it was put there because of me.

I know there are moments when it’s quieter in my mind, but I don’t really have days where I feel completely free of it. It’s more like I can push it aside for a bit, and then something small happens and it all comes back.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I came in because I’ve been having this feeling that I just can’t seem to shake, even when I’m having a pretty good day otherwise. It’s mostly around people from church, and also the walking club I go to. I keep feeling like certain people are watching me, not in an obvious way exactly, but like they’re keeping track of me.\n\nAnd then little things start to seem like they’re about me. Like something written in the church bulletin, or a comment someone makes in passing during the walk, and I’ll think, “That was meant for me,” or “They’re trying to send a message about me without saying it directly.” I know it probably sounds strange, and I’ve tried to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into things, but it keeps happening, and it’s been upsetting enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people who you feel are watching you are working together or is it more individual?\n\nPatient: It feels more like… connected, I guess. It’s like certain people at church and then certain people from the walking club seem to pick up on the same thing.\n\nLike I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a little note, and it feels like it’s meant for me, like they put it there so I’d know they were aware of me. And then later, during the walking group, someone will say something in passing that seems to line up with it, not direct enough that I could point to it and say, “There, that’s it,” but enough that I get that feeling again, like they’re watching me or keeping track.\n\nI know that sounds… I know how it sounds. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into it. I mean, I know other people might not understand why a church bulletin would feel like it’s talking about me, or why some offhand comment in the walking club would seem connected. If I tried to explain it to someone, they might say, “Well, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They were just making conversation.”\n\nBut to me, in the moment, it feels pointed. Like even on days when I’m doing all right otherwise, I can’t quite shake this feeling that certain people are watching me, sort of keeping tabs, and then those little things — a phrase in the bulletin, something someone says during the walk — feel like messages about me. That’s part of why I’m here, because I can hear how it might sound, but it still keeps feeling real to me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages you think are being sent to you are threatening in any way?\n\nPatient: Not threatening like they’re going to hurt me, exactly… I don’t usually think that. It’s more that it feels uncomfortable and pointed, like they’re watching me and trying to let me know they know things about me, or they’re talking about me without saying my name.\n\nLike with the church bulletin, it might just be a phrase or a little note, but I’ll read it and think, “That’s about me.” And then later someone in the walking club says something that seems to match up with it, and I get this sinking feeling, like it isn’t just coincidence. Sometimes it’s just a few words, not a long sentence. The strange part is it can sound like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or maybe the porch. It’s not muffled the way you’d think if I was just imagining it — it feels clear in that moment, like someone really called from the other room.\n\nThen I’ll go still and listen, and of course there’s no one there. I feel embarrassed even saying it, because I know how it sounds, but it’s happened enough times that I can’t just brush it off as a one-time mistake. It startles me more than anything. Sometimes I answer before I even have time to think, and then I feel silly standing there in the quiet.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear ever tell you to do something or influence your actions in any way?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of giving me instructions, no. It’s not like a voice says, “Go do this,” or “Don’t do that.” It’s more that I’ll hear my name, or just a few words, and it catches me off guard.\n\nUsually it’s when the house is very quiet, and I’ll think I hear my friend’s voice from the kitchen or the porch, clear enough that I might answer before I remember nobody is there. Like I’ll say, “What?” and then I’m standing there listening, and there’s nothing. That’s the part that bothers me, how real it seems for a second.\n\nI suppose it influences me only in that I stop what I’m doing and check, or I get a little unsettled afterward. But it doesn’t tell me to do things. It’s more startling than commanding.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of being watched and the messages you think you're receiving seem to be part of a larger story or idea that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s part of what scares me, because it does start to feel like it all fits together, even when I tell myself maybe I’m misunderstanding.\n\nIt’s mostly connected to people at church and in my walking club. On better days, I can still go and act normal enough, but I can’t shake this feeling that certain people are watching me more closely than they should be. Like they notice where I sit, who I talk to, whether I missed a Sunday, little things like that.\n\nAnd then I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a line that seems too close to something about me, and I think, “Why would they put that in there unless it was meant for me?” Or someone in the walking club will say something kind of casual, but the way they say it feels pointed, like there’s a hidden message underneath it. Not always direct, but enough that I feel like they’re trying to tell me they know things about me or they’re discussing me without saying it outright.\n\nI know if I say it out loud it sounds like maybe I’m reading too much into things. I do know that. But in the moment, it feels connected, like the bulletin and the comments and the watching are all part of the same thing. Even when I’m having a better day, I can’t completely talk myself out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or that messages are being sent to you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes I can distract myself for a little while, yes. If I’m busy with something very ordinary, like washing dishes or talking to someone about nothing important, I may not be thinking about it every minute.\n\nBut honestly, even on my better days, it’s still there in the background. That’s actually why I came in, because I can’t seem to fully shake this feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, sort of keeping track of me. And then little things feel like messages — something in the bulletin, or a comment someone makes while we’re walking — and I start thinking it was put there because of me.\n\nI know there are moments when it’s quieter in my mind, but I don’t really have days where I feel completely free of it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, I do. I don’t always notice it right away while I’m talking, but then I see the person’s face, like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t.

I’ll start off trying to explain one simple thing, like something about the mail or something that happened at church, and in my mind it all feels connected, so I keep going. But then somehow I’m talking about a quilt pattern I saw, or the birds outside my window, or how something felt like a warning, and I realize I’ve gone far away from where I started.

It’s frustrating because I know there’s a point I’m trying to make. I can feel the point, if that makes sense. But getting it out in a straight line is hard. I’ll be saying, “Well, the bulletin said this,” and then I remember the envelope in the mail, and then the colors on the quilt, and then the birds were all gathered in a way that seemed important, and by then the person is just looking at me like they don’t know what I’m getting at.

So yes, I think people understand pieces of what I say, but not always the whole thing. And then I get embarrassed and try to explain more, which sometimes makes it worse.
=====================================

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23, F28\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I came in because I’ve been having this feeling that I just can’t seem to shake, even when I’m having a pretty good day otherwise. It’s mostly around people from church, and also the walking club I go to. I keep feeling like certain people are watching me, not in an obvious way exactly, but like they’re keeping track of me.\n\nAnd then little things start to seem like they’re about me. Like something written in the church bulletin, or a comment someone makes in passing during the walk, and I’ll think, “That was meant for me,” or “They’re trying to send a message about me without saying it directly.” I know it probably sounds strange, and I’ve tried to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into things, but it keeps happening, and it’s been upsetting enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people who you feel are watching you are working together or is it more individual?\n\nPatient: It feels more like… connected, I guess. I don’t know that I can prove they’re all sitting down together and planning it, but it doesn’t feel completely random or just one person, either. It’s like certain people at church and then certain people from the walking club seem to pick up on the same thing.\n\nLike I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a little note, and it feels like it’s meant for me, like they put it there so I’d know they were aware of me. And then later, during the walking group, someone will say something in passing that seems to line up with it, not direct enough that I could point to it and say, “There, that’s it,” but enough that I get that feeling again, like they’re watching me or keeping track.\n\nI know that sounds… I know how it sounds. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into it. But when it happens in both places, church and the walking club, it’s hard not to feel like there’s some kind of connection between them.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages you think are being sent to you are in any way unusual or impossible for others to understand?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes and no. I mean, I know other people might not understand why a church bulletin would feel like it’s talking about me, or why some offhand comment in the walking club would seem connected. If I tried to explain it to someone, they might say, “Well, that’s just a normal announcement,” or “They were just making conversation.”\n\nBut to me, in the moment, it feels pointed. Like even on days when I’m doing all right otherwise, I can’t quite shake this feeling that certain people are watching me, sort of keeping tabs, and then those little things — a phrase in the bulletin, something someone says during the walk — feel like messages about me. Not loud or obvious, but like they’re meant for me to notice.\n\nI do realize it probably sounds unusual to someone else. That’s part of why I’m here, because I can hear how it might sound, but it still keeps feeling real to me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages you think are being sent to you are threatening in any way?\n\nPatient: Not threatening like they’re going to hurt me, exactly… I don’t usually think that. It’s more that it feels uncomfortable and pointed, like they’re watching me and trying to let me know they know things about me, or they’re talking about me without saying my name.\n\nLike with the church bulletin, it might just be a phrase or a little note, but I’ll read it and think, “That’s about me.” And then later someone in the walking club says something that seems to match up with it, and I get this sinking feeling, like it isn’t just coincidence. It doesn’t feel openly dangerous, but it does feel unsettling, like I’m being monitored or singled out.\n\nEven on days when I’m otherwise doing okay, I can’t shake it. So I wouldn’t say threatening in a direct way, but it does make me feel uneasy and guarded.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of being watched and receiving hidden messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about eleven months now, I think. Not quite a full year, but close enough that it feels like it’s been going on for a long time.\n\nAt first I wasn’t sure what to make of it, and I kept thinking maybe it was just me being too sensitive or reading things wrong. But the feeling of being watched, and then those messages or little signs that seem like they’re meant for me, that’s been happening for around eleven months. And during that same stretch, I’ve also had the voices and times where it’s harder to keep my thoughts straight, like I get tangled up trying to explain myself.\n\nSo, yes… I’d say roughly eleven months.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned having voices, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Yes… by voices, I don’t mean like a whole conversation going on all day or anything like that. It’s more… it happens sometimes when the house is very quiet, especially if I’m alone and there isn’t any television or music on.\n\nI’ll hear my name, clear enough that I look up or even answer, like, “What?” And then I remember nobody is there. Sometimes it’s just a few words, not a long sentence. The strange part is it can sound like my friend’s voice, like it’s coming from the kitchen or maybe the porch. It’s not muffled the way you’d think if I was just imagining it — it feels clear in that moment, like someone really called from the other room.\n\nThen I’ll go still and listen, and of course there’s no one there. I feel embarrassed even saying it, because I know how it sounds, but it’s happened enough times that I can’t just brush it off as a one-time mistake. It startles me more than anything. Sometimes I answer before I even have time to think, and then I feel silly standing there in the quiet.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear ever tell you to do something or influence your actions in any way?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of giving me instructions, no. It’s not like a voice says, “Go do this,” or “Don’t do that.” It’s more that I’ll hear my name, or just a few words, and it catches me off guard.\n\nUsually it’s when the house is very quiet, and I’ll think I hear my friend’s voice from the kitchen or the porch, clear enough that I might answer before I remember nobody is there. Like I’ll say, “What?” and then I’m standing there listening, and there’s nothing. That’s the part that bothers me, how real it seems for a second.\n\nI suppose it influences me only in that I stop what I’m doing and check, or I get a little unsettled afterward. But it doesn’t tell me to do things. It’s more startling than commanding.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of being watched and the messages you think you're receiving seem to be part of a larger story or idea that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s part of what scares me, because it does start to feel like it all fits together, even when I tell myself maybe I’m misunderstanding.\n\nIt’s mostly connected to people at church and in my walking club. On better days, I can still go and act normal enough, but I can’t shake this feeling that certain people are watching me more closely than they should be. Like they notice where I sit, who I talk to, whether I missed a Sunday, little things like that.\n\nAnd then I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a line that seems too close to something about me, and I think, “Why would they put that in there unless it was meant for me?” Or someone in the walking club will say something kind of casual, but the way they say it feels pointed, like there’s a hidden message underneath it. Not always direct, but enough that I feel like they’re trying to tell me they know things about me or they’re discussing me without saying it outright.\n\nI know if I say it out loud it sounds like maybe I’m reading too much into things. I do know that. But in the moment, it feels connected, like the bulletin and the comments and the watching are all part of the same thing. Even when I’m having a better day, I can’t completely talk myself out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or that messages are being sent to you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes I can distract myself for a little while, yes. If I’m busy with something very ordinary, like washing dishes or talking to someone about nothing important, I may not be thinking about it every minute.\n\nBut honestly, even on my better days, it’s still there in the background. And then little things feel like messages — something in the bulletin, or a comment someone makes while we’re walking — and I start thinking it was put there because of me.\n\nI know there are moments when it’s quieter in my mind, but I don’t really have days where I feel completely free of it. Like they notice where I sit, who I talk to, whether I missed a Sunday, little things like that.\n\nAnd then I’ll see something in the church bulletin, just a phrase or a line that seems too close to something about me, and I think, “Why would they put that in there unless it was meant for me?” Or someone in the walking club will say something kind of casual, but the way they say it feels pointed, like there’s a hidden message underneath it. Not always direct, but enough that I feel like they’re trying to tell me they know things about me or they’re discussing me without saying it outright.\n\nI know if I say it out loud it sounds like maybe I’m reading too much into things. I do know that. But in the moment, it feels connected, like the bulletin and the comments and the watching are all part of the same thing. Even when I’m having a better day, I can’t completely talk myself out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or that messages are being sent to you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes I can distract myself for a little while, yes. If I’m busy with something very ordinary, like washing dishes or talking to someone about nothing important, I may not be thinking about it every minute.\n\nBut honestly, even on my better days, it’s still there in the background. That’s actually why I came in, because I can’t seem to fully shake this feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, sort of keeping track of me. And then little things feel like messages — something in the bulletin, or a comment someone makes while we’re walking — and I start thinking it was put there because of me.\n\nI know there are moments when it’s quieter in my mind, but I don’t really have days where I feel completely free of it. It’s more like I can push it aside for a bit, and then something small happens and it all comes back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it difficult to express your thoughts in a way that feels clear and understandable to others?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I don’t always notice it right away while I’m talking, but then I see the person’s face, like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t.\n\nI’ll start off trying to explain one simple thing, like something about the mail or something that happened at church, and in my mind it all feels connected, so I keep going. But then somehow I’m talking about a quilt pattern I saw, or the birds outside my window, or how something felt like a warning, and I realize I’ve gone far away from where I started.\n\nIt’s frustrating because I know there’s a point I’m trying to make. I can feel the point, if that makes sense. But getting it out in a straight line is hard. I’ll be saying, “Well, the bulletin said this,” and then I remember the envelope in the mail, and then the colors on the quilt, and then the birds were all gathered in a way that seemed important, and by then the person is just looking at me like they don’t know what I’m getting at.\n\nSo yes, I think people understand pieces of what I say, but not always the whole thing. And then I get embarrassed and try to explain more, which sometimes makes it worse.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think all these things - the watching, the messages, the voices - are connected in some way that makes sense to you?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "patient_turn_index: 11\n(If 1, this is your first reply — return your chief complaint.)\nReturn the JSON now."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… the watching and the messages, those feel connected to me. That’s really why I came in, because even when I’m having what I’d call a better day, when I can still go out and be polite and do the normal things, I can’t shake the feeling that certain people at church and in the walking club are watching me.

It’s not like one big thing happened all at once. It’s more little things that add up. Someone notices whether I’m there or not, or looks at me a little too long, or says something during the walk that seems casual but feels like it has another meaning. And then the church bulletin will have a phrase in it that feels too close to something about me, and I start thinking, “That was put there for me to see.”

I know part of me can say maybe I’m making connections that aren’t really there. I do understand that’s possible. But it doesn’t feel that way when it’s happening. It feels like the watching and the little messages are part of the same thing, like people are keeping track of me and letting me know they know. That’s the part I can’t seem to talk myself out of.

---------- turn 11 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------
